1099. RICHARD THORETT was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of August, one jacket, value 3 s.; one pair of of trowsers, value 3 s.; one shawl, value 1 s.; one pair of stockings, value 6 d., and one cake of soap, value 18 d., the goods of John Burgess.
JOHN BURGESS. I live at Colebrook. On the 2d of August I expected a parcel from Mr. Biddlecome, it did not arrive. I found the prisoner at Eaton that day, with the jacket and trowsers on him. I saw him give a woman a pair of stockings before I got up to him.
THOMAS LEXTON. I am a constable of Stanmore. The prisoner was given into my charge, for stealing the bundle, he said voluntarily that he committed the robbery, but it was the first he ever did, and if he got through it, it would be a warning to him, and that the soap and shawl were hid in Ditton Park.
GEORGE BIDDLECOMBE. I am steward of the Naval Institution. I sent a parcel containing these things to the Old White Horse Cellar, directed to Burgess. I had given them to him.
JOHN LAMBERT. I am a waggoner. I had one parcel, and missed it at Colebrook. The prisoner went down in the waggon that day.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner. I was in distress.
GUILTY. Aged 43.
Confined Two Months.

1100. ANN WILLIAMS was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of June, forty-eight handkerchiefs, value 5 l,, the goods of Charles Dimes.
CHARLES DIMES. I am a haberdasher, and live in Holborn. On the 8th of June, the prisoner came to my shop, and said she was servant to the niece of Mrs. Collyer, of the Bull Inn, who was married, and lived in Northampton-square, and her mistress wished to see some handkerchiefs, that she was going out, and they must be sent directly. I looked out three or four dozen, and sent my daughter with them. They then went away together.
ELIZA DIMES. My father sent me with the prisoner. I had the parcel. When we got to the back of Merlin's Cave, public-house, she said
"This is our house, if you will stop a few minutes in the garden I will see if my master is at home." She spoke to a person at the bar, returned and said she did not know what to do, for her mistress was gone to her sister's. I agreed to go there. She said I could wait in the parlour till she returned. I gave them to her and sat down in Merlin's Cave. She did not return, and was apprehended two days afterwards. I gave them to her to shew her mistress, she was to bring me back what her mistress did not choose.
JOHN BARNLEY. I am a constable. I apprehended the prisoner, and found two duplicates in her pocket, and three more in a bureau in her room.
ALEXANDER MILNE. I am a pawnbroker. On the 9th of June, the prisoner pledged three handkerchiefs, and on the 10th two more.
JOHN WHELDEN. On the 8th of June, the prisoner pledged eight handkerchiefs with me.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was sent by Mr. Patterson for them, and gave them to him. He said he should send back what he did not keep.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Confined Six Months.
Third Middlesex Jury before H. R. Reynolds, Esq.

1102. GEORGE OLDHAM was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of July, one handkerchief, value 2 s., the goods of John Stephens, from his person.
JOHN STEPHENS. I am a merchant, and live in the Adelphi. On the 9th of July, about ten o'clock at night, I was coming along the Strand, felt something touch my pocket, looked round, and caught hold of the prisoner, who was immediately behind me, and took a handkerchief from his waistcoat, which was not mine. A person came up, and said, in his hearing, that he saw the prisoner give my handkerchief to another. I gave him in charge.
THOMAS GROVES. I am a sword-cutler. I was going down the Strand, and saw the prisoner in company with another. I saw the prisoner take the handkerchief from the prosecutor's coat-pocket, and give it to one of the others, who ran across the street. The prosecutor turned round, and I secured him. I saw it distinctly.
GUILTY. Aged 14.
Transported for Seven Years. - Recommended to Mercy.

1104. JOSEPH COLLINS was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of July, one watch, value 25 l.; two seals, value 9 l.; one key, value 1 l., and one ring, value 1 l., the goods of Samuel Verdy Read, from his person.
SAMUEL VERDY READ. I am a ship-owner in Jamaica. On the 21st of July, about nine o'clock in the evening, I was going by George-yard, with a lady on my left arm. The prisoner made a slip with his foot, and fell slightly with his head against my breast, at that instant he made a snatch at my watch, pulled it out, and ran up George-yard with it. I immediately called Stop thief! and received several violent blows from others. An officer knocked him down, and the case of the watch dropped from him immediately. I saw it fall from him. I never lost sight of him till he was secured.
SAMUEL MILLER. I am an officer. I was in George-yard, heard the cry of Stop thief! and stopped the prisoner who was running before all the rest, we both fell down, and at this instant the case fell on the ground, and the watch close to it. It fell from him.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Transported for Life.
Third Middlesex Jury, before H. R. Reynolds, Esq.

1109. THOMAS JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 3d. of July, one watch, value 3 l.; one seal, value 15 s., and one key, value 1 d., the goods of Edward Fricker, from his person.
EDWARD FRICKER. I am a tailor, and live at Bexley, in Kent. On the 3d of July I was with some friends in Portman-street at half-past three o'clock in the afternoon, the prisoner and several more stood at the curb. I was stopped by the crowd, and turned to go back. Then the prisoner and two or three more, closed upon us, and forced us back into the crowd. I said to my friend,
"Stand back a few minutes, and we shall be able to get back." The prisoner came before us, and in an instant put his hand down. I felt my watch move from the bottom of my fob, and saw him take it out. I immediately seized him, and he gave it to one of the others. They tried to rescue him, but I kept him, and Gall took him.
THOMAS GALL. I am a constable. The prosecutor gave the prisoner into my charge.
Prisoner's Defence. I was near him, and he collared me. I declare I am innocent.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Life.

1111. ROLAND TYLER was indicted for stealing, on the 2d of September, one seal, value 7 s., the goods of Lambert Gauron.
JOSEPH BOWDEN. I am servant to Mr. Lambert Gauron, who lives in Drury-lane. On the 2d of September, about one o'clock, the prisoner and another man came into the shop, he went outside the window, and pointed out two seals, which he wanted to see. I shewed him one, and said it was 5 s. - he returned it. I gave him the other into his hand, and asked 10 s. for it - he told me to brush another seal up. I went to get the leather and brush, and saw him put his hand behind him, and the other man put his hand into the prisoner's hand - he had not delivered me the other seal; my master came into the shop, then the prisoner's companion asked him what a dozen seals would be - he went out immediately - the prisoner then said,
"What can you do these seals at a dozen?" My master told him, and, I believe, he grumbled. I went to get another seal, and recollected what I had seen, and told my master, that he had not returned the seal I shewed him; he stopped the prisoner - he said he had given me the seal, which was false - the officer took him. I should know the other man again. I am sure they were companions.
LAMBERT GAURON. I came in while the prisoner and another man were in the shop, his companion said he had come about a man who was in trouble, and asked what I could make seals at per dozen? I said 3 l. 16 s.; he said very well, he would call again on Monday, and immediately left the shop. The prisoner looked at a seal, and asked me the lowest price - I said 5 s. I asked the boy to reach a seal from the window, at 10 s. 6 d., he said he had shewn the prisoner that seal, and told me what he had seen. The prisoner said if I would let him go, he would tell me where the man was who had got the seal.
CHRISTOPHER JACKSON. I am a constable. I took the prisoner in charge. He used great force to get away as I took him to Bow-street, and looked behind, as if he expected a companion.
Prisoner's Defence. The boy has been taken up several times for robbing his master.
LAMBERT GAURAN. I never charged him with robbing me.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Seven Years.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.

1016. JOHN THOMPSON was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of August, one spoon, value 7 s., the goods of the Earl of Morton.
SECOND COUNT. Stating it to belong to the Baron Douglas.
GEORGE PARKER. I am butler to the Earl of Morton. On the 29th of August, I was in the pantry, and laid the spoon in the window, which looks into the area. Between four and five o'clock in the afternoon, I saw the prisoner come down the area, and as he did not ring the bell I went to watch him, and saw him earnestly looking in at the window. Before I got to the hall door, I heard the pantry window open - ran back and saw his hand reaching in and take the spoon; I ran out to seize him - he threw the spoon down under the window and escaped - he had moved it about two feet. I followed him calling Stop thief! he knocked a man down, who tried to stop him. He was secured before I lost sight of him. He dropped it into the sink.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I heard the alarm, and ran to see what it was. I could not overtake him, and was stopped myself. He said I was the man, and had the spoon about me.
GEORGE PARKER. re-examined. I said so, as I saw it in his hand, and did not find it until I returned.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Transported for Seven Years.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.

1018. HARRIET MURRAY was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of August, one watch, value 3 l.; two seals, value 2 l., and one key, value 15 s., the goods of David Harrison.
DAVID HARRISON. On the 19th of August, about nine o'clock at night, I met the prisoner in St. James-street, and went with her to the Almonery. She left me before the morning, and as she had a handkerchief of mine I asked her to return, which she did at five o'clock in the afternoon. I then took out my watch; she said
"What are you afraid of?" I said,
"I am not afraid of you." She took it out of my hand, and went out with it. I slept there three nights, expecting her to return with it, but have never seen it since.
EDWARD HANDLEY. I am an officer. I apprehended the prisoner about a week before the robbery.
NOT GUILTY.

1019. FRANCIS ANDREWS was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of August, one coat, value 4 l., and one waistcoat, value 10 s., the goods of John Banks, from the person of William Clutterham .
WILLIAM CLUTTERHAM. I am servant to Mr. John Banks, a tailor, who lives in Bentinck-street. On the 12th of August, about eight o'clock at night, I was taking this coat and waistcoat to Mr. Winford, Searle's Coffee-house, at the corner of Carey-street; I saw the prisoner and another man; I asked them the way to Searle's Coffee-house, they said lower down. I went on and asked a gentleman who stood at a door. They came up and said they were going there, and would show me. At the corner of Hemlock-court, the prisoner touched his companion's arm, then snatched my bundle, and ran down the court. I hallooed Stop thief! and the prisoner was taken in three minutes with it.
ANDREW ROGERS. I live in Hemlock-court. I heard the alarm - the prisoner ran by - I jostled against him, and he dropped the bundle; I picked it up - he was stopped in a moment.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Transported for Life.

1020. JOHN PHILLIPS was indicted for stealing, on the 22d of August, one watch, value 3 l.; one ribbon, value 2 d., and one key, value 10 s., the goods of James Stone, from his person.
JAMES STONE. I am a tailor, and live in Skinner-street. On the 22d of August, about five o'clock in the afternoon, I was in King's-street, St. James-square - I stood clear of the mob. About ten young men rushed from the crowd, and snatched my watch from my fob; I believe the prisoner to be one of them. I saw it in about ten days, at Bow-street, through an advertizement.
THOMAS CLONEY. I am a wine-cooper. I was sitting at home at tea, in Great Windmill-street, between four and five o'clock in the afternoon, on the 2d of September, I took the prisoner on the charge of Mr. Sawyer. He produced a watch at the watch-house, and offered it to Mr. Sawyer, in place of his own. The prosecutor claimed it.
HENRY MONTAGUE. I am a footman. On the 2d of September, I assisted in taking the prisoner in St. James-square; on a charge of felony, and saw the watch found upon him.
WILLIAM SMITH. I am a patrol. I took charge of the prisoner at the watch-house. He produced the watch.
(Property produced, and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. A person who is gone to sea, pledged it in my name, and I redeemed it; the officer has a memorandum of it.
WILLIAM SMITH re-examined. No papers relating to it was found upon him. I took him to the pawnbroker's where he said he redeemed it. They indentified him as the man, who pledged and redeemed it.
GUILTY. Aged 28.
Transported for Life.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18200918-222]
1121. SARAH LYON was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of July, ten handkerchiefs, value 10 s.; three silver coins, value 2 s. 6 d., and two copper coins, value 1 d., the goods of Samuel Cohen.
MR. ANDREWS conducted the prosecution.
PRISCILLA COHEN. I am the wife of Samuel Cohen, who is a linen-draper, and lives at No. 168, Shoreditch. The prisoner was seven weeks in our service. I asked her one day for a scarf of mine, which was washed at home. I had told her to put it under my bed to press - she said she put it under my bed - she seemed flurried, and I went to look and found a parcel containing five handkerchiefs, under her own bed. (the scarf was under mine) I had seen them in the shop the night before. I told her of it, and turned her away. She was apprehended two days after.
Cross-examined by MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. How many of your daughters slept with the prisoner - A. One who is about fifteen years old, and a niece seventeen years old. I sent the prisoner away on Friday.
SAMUEL COHEN. On Friday, while the prisoner was in the house, my wife informed me of this. I told her to keep her till I was at leisure. I then told her that two bundles in the shop had been misplaced and rumpled in the night, and some cambric taken away. The handkerchiefs were mine. She said it was the first thing she ever took. I was called down to the shop. I said she must not go away without being searched. I saw her go out while I was busy. I sent for Levy on Saturday; as my father knew the girl to be a thief, and I missed other things. We found her at her sweetheart's apartments. She was searched and the coins found on her, which I swear to be mine. They were taken from my looking-glass drawer, they were marked in a particular manner by a stamp. It was done some years back. She was then taken into custody, but let go again, as her father said he knew where all the property was. She was apprehended again on Sunday.
Cross-examined. Q. How long were the handkerchiefs missed before you turned her out of doors - A. She was not turned out exactly. My wife said she should not sleep in the house. I had missed nothing but the handkerchiefs when she left. They were worth 3 s. each. I missed no more that day.
Q. Pray, how soon after did you write to Mrs. Gasheer to make you amends for the false character she gave her - A. About three-quarters of an hour, and before I missed any thing else. I said 200 l. or 300 l. would not compensate for what I had lost. I demanded no money of Mrs. Gasheer.
Q. When the coins were found on her, they were in your possession a day and a half afterwards - A. No. I kept them a few hours after, they were found on her on Sunday. I pointed the marks out when they were found, and gave them to the Magistrate on Monday, as the officer would not take them of me.
Q. Did she say your niece took them - A. She said so on Sunday.
MR. ANDREWS. Q. When you missed more property and told her of it, what did she say - A. She mentioned the person who was the instigator of it. Mrs. Gasheer is her aunt. The marks were on the coins before they were lost. Mr. Isaacs, the prisoner's attorney, attended before the Magistrate in my name without my authority.
MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. At the time Mr. Isaacs attended, was not the prisoner examined as a witness - A. I charged her with the robbery. One Jacobs was charged as the receiver.
COURT. Q. How long before had you marked the money - A. Some years. They were all marked with the same punch. I cannot find the punch now. This is the letter I wrote to Gasheer - (looking at it.)
SAMUEL LEVY. I am a constable. I apprehended the prisoner on Sunday the 16th in Roper's-buildings. I had searched her on Saturday at her father's. Her father and Cohen searched her, two shillings, a sixpence, and two halfpence were found on her. I have had them ever since two hours after she was in custody, when Cohen gave them to me - he kept them till then. He had said he would not prosecute her, or I should have taken her on Saturday.
- WARTERS. I produce the handkerchiefs which Cohen gave me at the office on Monday, as Levy had refused to take them, because they had been so long in Cohen's possession.
SAMUEL COHEN. They are mine.
Prisoner. I leave my defence to my Counsel.
Letter read.
"MR. GASHEER, - This is to inform you that I received a character of a servant from you, whom your wife said had lived two years with you, and that she allowed her to receive money. Now she has robbed me, and you know she has given me a false character. I expect you will make good the property I lost. I shall proceed against you for it, as my solicitor directs.
S. COHEN."
SAMUEL LEVY re-examined. When the coin was found on the prisoner, I do not recollect Cohen saying he knew them by any mark.
NOT GUILTY.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18200918-223]
1122. JESSE BOWTELL was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of August, one watch, value 1 l.; one chain, value 10 s.; one seal, value 5 s., and two keys, value 5 s., the goods of Thomas Ingram, from his person.
THOMAS INGRAM. I live in Harley-street. On the 18th of August I was standing at the entrance to Spring Gardens to see her Majesty pass. A rush came instantly at me. The prisoner came with his arms and held against me with great force. I felt my watch go, and saw the chain hanging from his hand. I seized and kept him till the officer took him. Some gentleman produced my watch.
Cross-examined by MR. ANDREWS. Did not the gentleman say,
"Here is your watch, you have got the wrong man" - A. No. The prisoner dragged me into the middle of the road.
WILLIAM TATE. I am a tea-dealer, and live in the City-road. I saw the prosecutor rush towards the prisoner, and saw the chain in his hand. I attempted to seize him, but five or six came round, cried Rescue, and threw me into the middle of the road. I saw a gentleman take the watch from the prisoner's hand, and give it to Mr. Ingram.
JAMES HAUDLEY. I am a constable. Immediately as the Queen's carriage passed I saw a general rush. There was a cry for an officer. I saw two or three gentlemen struggling with the prisoner, and secured him. Several men endeavoured to rescue him. One of the gentlemen who had hold of him gave me the watch. He made great resistance, and it was with difficulty I could secure him.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was taken to a public-house. The watch was brought in by a man.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Life.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before J. Vaillant, Esq.
[trialAccount t18200918-224]
1123. THOMAS JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 3d of July, one coat, value 1 l., the goods of Robert Patterson, Esq.
ROBERT HUNT. I was servant to Mr. Robert Patterson, who lives in York-place. I went up to answer a knock at the door; in going down again heard somebody in the passage, and missed the coat from the pantry. The prisoner was brought back with it.

JOHN MANDER. I am a coachman to General Clinton. I stopped the prisoner about twenty yards from the prosecutor's, in the street - a person who was cleaning a window opposite pointed him out to me. He threw the coat down and ran. I followed and secured him.

1125. JOHN MANNEY was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of July, five pair of shoes, value 30 s., the goods of John Chalmers.
THOMAS DUNKLEY. I am a shoemaker, and live in Middle-row, Broad-street, On the 25th of July I bought a pair of shoes of one Summer, in Rag Fair, he is not here, and on the 27th I bought three other pairs of him; I also bought another pair of Bumpus, whom I saw buy them of Summer.
JOHN FORRESTER. I apprehended the prisoner, and found two pair of shoes at his lodgings.
JOHN CHALMERS. I am a shoemaker, and live in Holborn, the prisoner was my servant. The two pair of shoes are mine; I do not know when I lost them. I believe the prisoner never bought them. My son has superintended my business for seven years. They are of a particular make, but have no shop-mark on them.
Cross-examined by MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. Where were they made - A. At Northampton. The same person makes for other people.
Q. You had notice to produce your books here - A. Yes, but I have not brought them, as they would prove nothing.
NOT GUILTY.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before J. Vaillant, Esq.

1126. NATHANIEL BRIGGS was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of July, one piece of nankeen, value 1 s., the goods of William Luke.

[trialAccount t18200918-228]
1127. WILLIAM M'VILLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of August, one hat, value 22 s.; one brush, value 9 d., and 17 s. 3 d. in monies numbered, the property of James Pitt.
SECOND COUNT, stating it to belong to William Yate.
WILLIAM YATES. I manage the business of Mr. James Pitt, who has a hatter's shop in Oxford-street. On the 12th of August the prisoner ordered a hat to be sent to Mr. Bond, No. 12, Colvill-court, Charlotte-street, Rathbone-place - the hat came to 22 s., and a brush to 9 d. I sent them by Wood, and gave him 3 d. for change. He brought them back, and asked for change of a 2 l. note. I gave him 17 s. in addition to the 3 d.; he returned with two 1 l. notes of the Milston-street Bath Bank, made payable at No. 11, Crutched Friars. I sent them for payment two or three days after, he brought them back. I had ordered Wood not to leave the goods without the money. On the 14th I went to Colville-court, and found no Mr. Bond lived there.
Cross-examined by MR. ANDREWS. Q. Did you receive the order from the prisoner - A. Yes, he wrote his name as Bond - he tried three hats on, and said it was for his brother at Romford. I am not in partnership with Mr. Pitt, but merely his agent.
THOMAS WOOD. I am errand-boy to Mr. Pitt; Yates sent me with a hat and box to Mr. Bond, No. 12, Colville-court - he gave me 3 d., and told me not to leave them without the money. I went to Colville-court, and met the prisoner at the door - I did not knock; I am certain it was him. He said,
"My little man, is that hat for me?" I said,
"Yes, Sir." He asked if I had change for 2 l.? I said I had only 3 d. He said,
"Run back, you may take the hat with you or leave it here; you have no need to be afraid, I will not run away - make haste, as I am in a hurry." I ran back with the hat, got 17 s., and found him at the corner of the court. He said,
"You have made haste, here is 3 d for you." I gave him 17 s. and he gave me two notes - I brought Mr. Yates the same notes, he did not approve of them. I went a day or two after to No. 11, Crutched Friars; I found no such persons as Sampson and Brothers, nor had they lived there for ten years.
Cross-examined. Q. They never sent you to Bath - A. No. There was a man tried at Clerkenwell for an offence at this house.
MR. ANDREWS, on behalf of the prisoner, called
MARY PHILLIMORE. I live at No. 12, Colville-court. A man about the prisoner's height called about some lodgings, I agreed to take him if his character answered, but he never came again. While he was in my house this lad brought a hat, the man went to the door; Wood had knocked at the door between two and three o'clock, he said he had brought a hat for the man, the man went to the door to him - it was not the prisoner - he was a country-looking man. I left them at the door, and did not see the boy give him the hat.
Prisoner. Q. Did you not see him give him the hatbox - A. Yes, I saw the man with the box when he left me.
Prisoner. I declare my innocence - they both said I was not the man. There is a man now at Sheerness who committed the offence, and has confessed it.
THOMAS WOOD re-examined. I did not go into the house. I found the prisoner at the door, and am certain I did not knock at the door at all. When I returned with the change he was at the corner of the court. I afterwards took the notes back to get them changed, I knocked at the door then, and Mrs. Phillimore let me in - I had partedwith the hat before this. She said the gentleman only came to take a lodging. I had not been in the house before, but when he ordered the hat he said if he should be out his wife would pay me. When I saw the prisoner at the corner of the court he went to the door, stood with one foot on the step, and paid me. There was another man standing in the court, leaning against a post. I am positive the prisoner is the man - I never doubted it.
GUILTY. Aged 29.
Transported for Seven Years.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before J. Vaillant, Esq.
[trialAccount t18200918-229]
1128. JOHN SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of July, one shawl, value 15 s., and one watch, value 15 s., the goods of John Wright.
JOHN WRIGHT. I am a shoemaker, and live in Samuel-street, St. George's in the East. I called Stop thief! a constable collared him, and I took the watch out of his right-hand pocket - the shawl was found on him.

1133. SUSAN RICHARDSON was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of July, one handkerchief, value 3 s., the goods of Thomas Pizzey, from his person.
THOMAS PIZZEY. I am a cabinet-maker, and live in Old Nicholl-street, Bethnal-green. On the 1st of August, about half-past one in the morning, I was in Shoreditch. I was very tired, and fell asleep on a step. I was quite sober, I was awoke by feeling somebody at my neck, jumped up, and saw the prisoner in the act of putting my neck-handkerchief into her bosom. I seized her, and took it from her. I found my coat-pockets turned out, she cried out Murder! and I was surrounded by about a dozen men, who struck me, and told me to let her go. I held her till the watchman took her. They then made off.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
WILLIAM HUGHES. I am a watchman. I heard the cryof watch, ran up, and found a dozen or more round the prosecutor, who was holding the prisoner. He gave charge of her, and gave the account he has now.
Prisoner's Defence. He took me home, and gave me the handkerchief instead of money.
PIZZEY. It is false.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Transported for Life.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before J. Vaillant, Esq.

1134. THOMAS SHAW was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of August, one watch, value 4 l.; one ribbon, value 6 d.; two rings, value 6 d., and one key, value 6 d., the goods of Henry Cotton, from his person.
HENRY COTTON. I am a cabinet-maker, and live in St. Martin's-street. About five o'clock in the afternoon I was at the end of Waterloo-place, the Queen was passing. My hands were pinioned down by a gang, who hustled me against the carriage, and I felt my watch go from my fob, About a quarter past six o'clock, I saw them taking a thief to the watch-house. I went in, and enquired for my property, I described it, it was produced to me, and I found the prisoner in custody. I remembered seeing the prisoner near me.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GEORGE DORRELL. I am a brush-maker, and live in Broad-street, Manchester-square. On the 24th of August, I was in St. James's-square, I saw two suspicious characters leaving the square, and at the bottom of Whitaker-street, the prisoner joined them, and shewed them something. I followed them, met three constables, and pointed them out. The prisoner saw me, immediately ran off, and I followed him into St. Martin's-lane, pushed him into a coffee-shop, and asked what he had in his pocket, as he put his hand in. He said
"Come none of your tricks. I have been robbed of 5 s. 6 d." He had got a watch half out of his pocket, I took it quite out, he said it was his own. I took him to the watch-house. The prosecutor claimed it in the evening.
Prisoner's Defence. The watch was thrown down, a man scrambled with me for it. I intended to give it up.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Life.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before J. Vaillant, Esq.

I informed the watchman next day, and he took her.
PATRICK CRAWLEY. I am a watchman. I saw Mr. Reusch in the passage of a house in Charles-street. He said he had been robbed, and described the girl; he was sober. I found his umbrella in a room on the first-floor, about four o'clock next morning with the prisoner. She lived on the first floor.
WILLIAM READY. I am a watchman. I apprehended the prisoner on the second pair front room. The umbrella was not there.
JOSEPH TRIMBY. I found the umbrella in the room; the prisoner was in bed.
(Umbrella produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. He gave me the umbrella.

1137. JOHN HALL was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of September, one watch, value 2 l., and one seal, value 2 s., the goods of Hezekiah Shepherd, from his person.
HEZEKIAH SHEPHERD. I am an assistant at a school. On the 13th of September, about ten o'clock in the morning, I was in the Commercial-road. A procession was going to her Majesty. I felt my watch drawn from my pocket, and saw the prisoner running two or three yards from me. I followed, called Stop thief! he ran about twenty yards off, without my losing sight of him, - he struggled to get away. The watch was dropped close by where I stopped him.
HENRY WHITE. I am a ribbon-weaver, and live in Quaker-street. I was in the Commercial-road, and picked the watch up in the kennel, just by the prisoner, and delivered it to the prosecutor.
JOHN STARKEY. I am an officer. The prosecutor gave the prisoner in charge for stealing his watch, he begged his pardon, and said,
"You have got your watch, let me go."
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I did not take it, others were running as well as me.
GUILTY. Aged 28.

1138. JOSEPH BRYAN was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of August, one handkerchief, value 3 s., the goods of a man unknown, from his person.
JAMES WAYLING. I am a constable. I was in St. James's-square, and saw the prisoner in company with a gang of about twenty, going along by the side of the Queen's carriage. I saw him clap his right hand quickly into his bosom. I immediately seized him, and took the handkerchief out of his bosom. Furzeman collared him also, and brought him away.
JOHN FURZEMAN. I am a constable. I saw twenty or thirty thieves with the prisoner. I saw him follow a gentleman in the crowd, pull his handkerchief out, and put it into his bosom. I lost the gentleman in the crowd, there was so many of them, that it was difficult to get the prisoner out. He said he picked it up; that was impossible, he could not pick it up in the crowd.
Prisoner's Defence. The handkerchief was thrown on my arm, two or three people wished to take it off - I did take it off.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Life.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.

JOHN BUTCHER. I am a carpenter. I was in the square facing Mr. Napier, and saw him seize the prisoner round the waist. I saw the watch at his heels. I forced myself down and got it.
WILLIAM HURST. I live in Richards's-buildings, Shirelane. I was with Mr. Napier, he missed his watch, we both seized the prisoner. I saw him drop it.

1148. WILLIAM PATTERSON was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of July, 30 lbs. of paper, value 10 s., the goods of the Directors and Guardians of the Poor, of St. Marylebone.
THREE OTHER COUNTS, stating it to belong to different persons.
MR. ADOLPHUS conducted the prosecution.
JOHN THORP. I am messenger to the Directors and Guardians of the Poor of St. Marylebone. These papers were deposited in the work-house, in a loft over the passage. The prisoner was a pauper there.
JOHN MILLER. I am a cheesmonger, and live in Dorset-street, St. Marylebone. About the begining of July the prisoner brought me these papers to sell in small quantities. He said it was waste paper, which Mr. Watts had flung aside, and bid him take it away.

1149. THOMAS DAY was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of May, 140 lbs. of paper, value 50 s., the goods of the Directors and Guardians of the Poor, of St. Marylebone; and WILLIAM CAMPLIN, was indicted for feloniously receiving the same, knowing them to be stolen.
JOHN THORP. Day was also a pauper, and Camplin sold sweetmeats about the street. He was apprehended, and said Day gave it him to sell. Day said it was a bad job, and he was sorry for it.
ELEASER HAMSON. I keep a chandler's shop, in Wellington-street. Camplin has sold me paper, for the last fifteen months. One parcel weighed twenty-five pounds.
RICHARD COATES. I took Day, and apprehended Camplin at his lodgings, in Park-street. The covers of the books, were found at his house.
THOMAS DAY - GUILTY. Aged 59.
Confined One Year.
WILLIAM CAMPLIN - NOT GUILTY.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18200918-251]
1150. ISAAC WOLFE, SAMUEL WHERRY, and DANIEL EDWARDS were indicted for stealing, on the 15th of July, three seals, value 1 l.; part of a chain, value 6 d., and one key, value 2 d., the goods of John Teasdale, from his person.
JOHN TEASDALE. I am clerk to Messrs Crosby and Co. St. John-street. On the 15th of July, between twelve and one o'clock in the day, I was walking along Devonshire-street, Portland-road; I saw the glass-blowers' procession, and stopped to look at them. A person who is not apprehended, came up and hit me a blow in my neck; I should know him again - I felt it for some moments. At the same time he snatched at my watch; the chainbroke, and he got the seals and key; he then handed it either to Edwards or Wherry, which I cannot say - they were immediately behind him in his company. I seized the man who struck me, and called for assistance. I was beset by several of them, and had stones and other things thrown at me - I still held the man until I was knocked down by several - I saw Wolfe among them. When they had partly dispersed, I got up and found several more hustling me to get my pocket-book. I got away as quick as I could - I was obliged to escape through a gateway into a marble-yard for safety. In an hour or two, I saw Edwards and Wherry among a crowd near White Conduit-house - I pointed them out to Smith; they were then secured. I seized Edwards myself, and gave him to Smith - they both ran away as they saw me with Smith and other officers - they were secured near thirty yards off. When I was in Portland-road, I had a second scuffle with the man I at first seized, and who had been rescued from me. I was struck at several times, and saw Wherry about the crowd. While I was being hustled, I felt a hand in my coat-pocket; as soon as I could turn round, I saw Wolfe was nearest behind me, and several loose papers about his feet, which were taken from my pocket. I was very much beaten. He was apprehended the next day. The second attack was twenty or thirty yards from where the first was.
Cross-examined by MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. Whether Wherry or Edwards took the seals from the man, you cannot say - A. No, he extended his hand towards them. They were among the crowd while I was being beat - they threw stones at me; one of the stones hit me in the neck. They were along with the man at the time he robbed me.
JAMES SMITH. I am keeper of St. Pancras watch-house. I was at White Conduit-house, and saw Mr. Teasdale at the top of the hill; he pointed out Edwards and Wherry among a gang of nine or ten. Edwards turned round and looked, and the whole gang ran off different ways. I followed and secured Edwards - he made great resistance. I told him he was a pickpocket - he then fought very hard, and one of those behind me struck me. As soon as I got hold of him, he whipped his hand into his pocket. I took two seals and a broken ring out of his hand; they have not been owned; he was trying to throw them away. I took him to the watch-house, and the other two were delivered to me.
WILLIAM COLTON. I am a broker. A gentleman was robbed on the hill near White Conduit-house. While another of the gang was being taken to the watch-house, some persons came up and said they had been robbed. I went to White Conduit-house, and we took fifteen pickpockets.
ANDREW SIRKE. I am a vellum-binder, and live in Field-lane. I came up the Portland-road directly after Mr. Teasdale was robbed, and followed him down the road to White Conduit-house. I first caught sight of Wherry and Edwards about a quarter of a mile off, in company together with several others. I saw Smith searching Edwards, and assisted in taking some of the gang.
GEORGE TAYLOR. I am a constable. Wherry and Edwards passed me at the corner of Cumming-street, when I was going to Clerkenwell with a prisoner. As I returned I assisted in securing a gang of fifteen, among whom were the prisoners.
JOSEPH CADBY. I assisted in securing the gang. The prisoners were among them.
JOHN MILLS. I am a constable. I received the whole gang in custody, and found a small necklace on Wherry.
WILLIAM JORDON. I assisted in securing the gang; they were being taken into a public-house, a mob of about 150 came round, and threatened to pull the house down.
EDWARDS'S Defence. The seals are mine.
ISAAC WOLFE - GUILTY. Aged 15.
SAMUEL WHERRY - GUILTY. Aged 16.
DANIEL EDWARDS - GUILTY. Aged 16.
Transported for Life.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18200918-252]

1154. CHARLES SMITH and GEORGE MASON were indicted for stealing, on the 28th of August, one watch, value 5 l.; two seals, value 2 s.; one chain, value 2 s., and one ring, value 10 s., the goods of Robert Chapman, from his person.
ROBERT CHAPMAN. I am a cabinet-maker, and live in Ashley-street. On the 28th of August, about four o'clock, I was opposite Carlton-house; the Queen's carriage was coming by. I stood against the iron-rails, and in half a minute I was surrounded by a gang, and saw my son knocked down. I went to his assistance, and lost my watch. I do not know either of the prisoners.
GEORGE TAYLOR. I am a constable. I saw a mobround the Queen's carriage; the prisoners were among them. There was a general fight between the prosecutor's son and Smith. A person gave Smith to me; I took him to the watch-house, it was said a man in red hair, was in his company. I went directly and saw Mason in Pall Mall, and told him he was wanted; he said he would go with me, and walked by my side to the watch-house.
WILLIAM MASON. I am a stationer, and live in Chapel-court, Swallow-street. I was with the officer, and saw Chapman by Carlton House. About a dozen of the gang got round and hustled him. I did not see the watch taken, but saw it in Smith's hand - he was close enough to take it. A person with red hair was in the crowd; I am not certain whether it was Mason; I had seen them in company before the robbery. I afterwards assisted in taking Smith out of the crowd. I saw Mr. Chapman's son endeavouring to take Mason. I rather think Smith struck him in the mouth. I saw him pass the silver watch to another man.
JOHN CHAPMAN. I am the prosecutor's son. We drew aside when the carriage came up. My attention was drawn to a man with red hair about a yard off; he could not take the watch. I saw him attempt another person's pocket, and I tried to seize him; he drew me out, I was knocked down and he was rescued. I will not say it was Mason, as I have seen two persons so much like him since. Smith knocked me down while I held the man.
WILLIAM JORDAN. I saw Smith strike Chapman a violent blow, and I secured him.
NOT GUILTY.
Third Middlesex Jury, before J. Vaillant, Esq.
[trialAccount t18200918-256]
1155. CHARLES MAPP was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of August, one watch, value 2 l., and one key, value 2 d., the goods of John Carr, from his person.
ANN BODY. I live at Camden-row, Bethnal-green-road. On the 19th of August, at four o'clock in the afternoon, I was walking with Mr. John Carr on Bethnal-green, and was met by the prisoner and three more. As the prisoner passed he snatched Mr. Carr's watch from his fob. They all ran off except one, who tried to hinder me from pursuing the prisoner, but I did, and saw him take his hat off. I ran about a hundred yards. He was not out of my sight while I followed. Stevenson pursued the same man and took him. I saw the prisoner full five minutes before he came up, remembered him, and can swear to him. Stevenson and others brought him back in a quarter of an hour. The watch was produced before that. Mr. Carr is eighty-seven years old, his sight is very bad, and he knows nothing about it.
Cross-examined by MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. Did not you say you knew him by his dress - A. No; I knew him by his features and his person.
CHARLES STEVENSON. I am a butcher, and live at Bethnal-green. I was in the shop, and heard Body call Stop thief! She was pursuing the prisoner. I ran by her and met a gentleman who said
"There he goes." He had turned round by the Greyhound. I saw him before he turned the corner. He was still running alone. I followed her to Green-street. He turned up North-place. I went a shorter way round, and met him at the end of North-place. He was then walking but very much heated and out of breath. I am sure he is the man. He said,
"Have you got him?" I laid hold of him and said,
"Yes, I have got him now." I did not exactly know what he had been doing, and as nobody came to my assistance I let him go. He turned short round and came back into Green-street as hard as he could. Before he had got to the bottom of North-place a person came up and said he had stolen a watch. I instantly followed him, he crossed Green-street, and I found him in custody in about five minutes. Some brick-makers had detained him till I came. I am certain he is the man. He strongly denied every thing, and said he ran because the people called stop thief. Directly Mrs. Body saw him, she said he was the man, and gave him in charge.
MR. JAMES CROWDER. I am an officer in the army. I was passing on the green when I heard the cry of stop thief, and saw Mrs. Body and four young men; one ran off and she pursued him. I followed and saw him take his hat off and wipe his face, and in putting it on again he threw his hand over the fore-court as if he threw something away. I lost sight of him. I went to the spot, and on the railing I found the watch; it had opened in falling. Wild took it up and delivered it to Body.
THOMAS WILD. I live at Bethnal-green. I picked the watch up under the railings of an empty house.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Life.
Third Middlesex Jury, before J. Vaillant, Esq.
[trialAccount t18200918-257]

Johnson came up, and took the child's necklace off, I secured him. He gave his hand a sharp jerk, and I have not seen the necklace since. THOMAS BURRIDGE. I am a constable. I saw a crowd, went up, and took James; he said he was with Johnson.

1162. HENRY WRIGHT and JOHN MATTHEWS were indicted for stealing, on the 16th of July, one handkerchief, value 4 s., the goods of a certain man unknown, from his person.
WILLIAM BIDGOOD. I am a carpenter, and live in Castle-street, Oxford-market. On the 16th of July, about nine o'clock at night, I was in the narrow part of Spring-gardens, going into the Park, and saw the prisoners together, feeling several gentlemens' pockets. They followed a gentleman along Cockspur-street, and at the corner of Pall-mall Wright took up the skirt of his coat, and took a handkerchief out. I went up, told the gentleman, and seized them both. Matthews threw the handkerchief down, and wrested himself from me; I picked it up, called Stop thief! Gillon stopped him in my sight. The gentleman claimed the handkerchief. The gang came round, I told him to follow me to the watch-house, but he did not. I do not know his name.
JOSEPH GILLON. My father is the watch-house-keeper. I heard Bidgood cry Stop thief! saw Matthews running in the road, and secured him.
JOHN RAYMENT. I am a smith. I saw Matthews running and seized him - on going up Church-passage he dropped a silk handkerchief.
WRIGHT - GUILTY. Aged 15.
MATTHEWS - GUILTY. Aged 16.
Transported for Life.
Third Middlesex Jury, before J. Vaillant, Esq.
[trialAccount t18200918-264]
1163. EMMA SMITH and MARY BYRNE were indicted for stealing, from the person of Henry Fielding Corfe, two 10 l., one 5 l., and three 1 l. Bank notes, his property.
HENRY FIELDING CORFE. I keep a school at New Church, Isle of Wight. On the 6th of July I lodged at the Nag's Head, James-street, Covent-garden. About half-past eleven o'clock at night I was going to my lodgings, the prisoners accosted me in Brydges-street, and solicited something to drink. I wished them to pursue a better course, Smith said she should be very happy to do so, but was afraid her friends would not take her back. I walked with them, conversing on that subject, and then went to their lodgings in a court near the Theatre, I might have remained there three or four hours. I sat on the bed, and being particularly tired, I fell asleep - Byrne had left me with Smith. I had taken my coat off, the notes were in the pocket. I awoke about four o'clock, and missed them, Smith was then gone. I got Gooding, and we found the prisoners together in Wych-street - I am certain they are the women - we secured them, and took them to a house. As Gooding was going up stairs with Smith, something fell from her; we called for a light, and found, a half handkerchief lying on the ground, containing my notes; they have my writing on them. We charged her with dropping them; she cried, but said nothing.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. How long did your moral lecture continue - A. I do not know; I merely accompanied her home. I was quite sober. I am a married man.
THOMAS GOODING. I am an officer. I went with Mr. Corfe, and took the prisoner in Wych-street. I took Smith, and he took Byrne. As I was taking Smith up stairs in a coffee-shop, something fell from her. I found the handkerchief, containing two 10 l., one 5 l., and one 1 l. note, with three half-crowns and one shilling. She said she was sorry for it, and hoped he would forgive her.
SMITH - GUILTY. Aged 16.
Fined One Shilling, and Discharged.
BYRNE - NOT GUILTY.
Third Middlesex Jury, before J. Vaillant, Esq.
[trialAccount t18200918-265]
1164. ALEXANDER READ was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of August, one watch, value 3 l.; one chain, value 1 l.; two seals, value 1 l., and two keys, value 3 d., the goods of John Jones, from his person.
JOHN JONES. I am a milkman, and live in Tower-street, Westminster-road. On the 31st of August, about half-past eleven o'clock in the morning, I was in Parliament-street, her Majesty was coming past; I was pressed and pinioned. I put my hands down, missed my watch, and found the prisoner in custody. My watch was produced.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
WILLIAM JONES. I am an officer. I saw the prisoner in company with two others, whom I knew before. I watched them from the corner of Parliament-street, they attempted to take every gentleman's watch who came near them. I saw the other two get on each side of Jones, the prisoner stood in front, and drew his watch out; I seized him and took it from him as he was putting it into his pocket. About forty thieves surrounded us, and if it had not been for the Bow-street officers we could not have kept him. I found a handkerchief on him, which he said was marked, but it was not.
WILLIAM MASON. I was with Jones, and confirm his statement.
GUILTY. Aged 15.
Transported for Life.
Third Middlesex Jury, before J. Vaillant, Esq.

1165. RACHAEL SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of July, one watch, value 4 l.; one seal, value 3 l., and one slide, value 5 s., the goods of Henry Godwin, from his person.
HENRY GODWIN. I am a land-waiter, and live in Mile End-road. On the 6th of July, about ten o'clock at night, I was in Stepney-fields, the prisoner came up and asked me to accompany her across the fields, as she was fearful - I did so. She said she was a servant out of place, and was going to Southend, but the woman where she lodged would not let her have her clothes as she owed her 2 s. 5 d. I was going to give her 2 s. 6 d., when she asked the time? I said I thought it was a quarter past ten o'clock. She said,
"I must wish you a good night, as I have an appointment at half-past." She immediately ran away, and in about ten minutes I missed my watch. I met a watchman, and told him. I found her at the watch-house with my watch.
ROBERT BLAKE. I am a watchman. About a quarter before eleven o'clock the prisoner came to me in Fieldgate-street, and said she had a watch, which she meant to pledge - I had known her by her walking the streets. She said she got it out of Stepney-fields. I asked her for it, she gave it to me, and I took her to the watch-house.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. The watchman wanted me to sell it to him.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Judgment Respited. - Sent to the Refuge for the Destitute
Third Middlesex Jury, before J. Vaillant, Esq.

1166. CHARLES SAMUEL SAVORY was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of August, two pair of trowsers, value 35 s., and one handkerchief, value 1 s., the goods of Charles Read, from his person.
CHARLES READ. I am a tailor, and live in Newman-street. On the 9th of August, a little after eleven o'clock at night, I was at the corner of George-street, St. Giles's, with two pair of trowsers in a bundle. I saw the prisoner and two others at the opposite corner; he crossed directly over to me, snatched the bundle from under my arm, and ran off with it - I followed, calling Stop thief! His companions crosed between us to hinder me, but I kept my eye on him - he ran away with a number of them. I was afraid to go in after him - he was stopped in a minute. My bundle was conveyed away. I am positive he is the man, for I did not lose sight of him.
LEWIS ROBERTS. I am a watchman. I heard the cry, saw a crowd running, and went in a direction to meet them, the prisoner was the foremost; I laid hold of him, Read appeared immediately, and said he was the man.
Prisoner's Defence. If he saw me take it he can say what I did with it.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Transported for Life.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.

1167. JANE MATTHEWS and SARAH RICHARDS were indicted for stealing, on the 27th of July, 24 pieces of velveteen, value 20 s., and 30 buttons, value 9 d., the goods of Daniel Courtney, from the person of Sarah Walton .
SECOND COUNT, stating them to be the property of Thomas Pearson.
ANN PEARSON. I live in Underwood-street, Mile End. I sent Sarah Walton with a bag to fetch these things from Courtney's, about two o'clock; she returned about three, with the bag empty.
SARAH WALTON. I am nine years old. Mrs. Courtney gave me this work in a bag, it was velveteen for breeches. As I came across the fields the prisoners followed me; Matthews knocked it out of my hand, and both ran away. I ran and screamed, and a man stopped Matthews, Richards got away, but was taken that night - I am sure of them both.
MARIA COURTNEY. I am the wife of Daniel Courtney, who is a tailor. I gave this child the materials for two pair of breeches, she put them in a canvas bag, and left about three o'clock - she went across the fields. I live inStoray-street. I found the prisoners in custody, with the property.
CHARLES EARL. I am a smith. I was in the fields, and saw the prisoners about two hundred yards from the child. One said to the other,
"Come, or we shall be too late." They went up to the child, Richards pushed her off the bank, and Matthews took the bundle. The child scrambled out of the ditch and ran after them screaming. I stopped Matthews with the property.
JOHN DOWNES. I am a constable. I took Matthews in charge. She told me where to find Richards, and I took her in about an hour.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
MATTHEWS'S Defence. Richards had nothing to do with it.
MATTHEWS - GUILTY. Aged 16.
Transported for Seven Years.
RICHARDS - GUILTY. Aged 15.
Judgment Respited. - Sent to the Refuge.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.

1171. ELIZABETH M'KENZIE and MARY WILLIAMS were indicted for stealing, on the 11th of July, at St. James, Clerkenwell, twenty-three yards of lace, value 1 l. 3 s., the goods of James Sawyer, privately in his shop.
EDWARD MILLARD. I am shopman to Mr. James Sawyer, who is a haberdasher, and lives in Goswell-street-road, in the parish of St. James's, Clerkenwell. On the 11th of July, between two and three o'clock in the afternoon, M'Kenzie came into the shop. Another customer was there. Only myself was serving. The other customer left the shop almost immediately, and another came in. M'Kenzie asked to see some common lace; I handed her some; none suited her; I shewed her several cards. The other customer being in a hurry, I reached her some satin which was behind me; I turned round to do so - she bought some. I then turned to the prisoner again; she said none of the lace suited her and left the shop; I had observed nothing, and had not seen Williams. About twenty minutes after the officer brought the prisoner in, and enquired if I had lost any lace. From the quantity we have I could not tell. He produced twenty-three yards which I knew by the shop-mark. M'Kenzie had bought none. The lowest value is 11 d. or 1 s. a yard.
THOMAS THOMPSON. I am a constable. On the 11th of July I was in St. John-street, and saw the prisoners in company about a quarter of a mile from Sawyer's shop. I watched them into five different shops; sometimes one went in, and at others the other. I followed them to Sawyer's. M'Kenzie went in and Williams stood at the corner of the window. I could not see what M'Kenzie was doing. She came out and joined Williams - they walked away. I apprehended them about two hundred yards off, and found the lace in M'Kenzie's apron. Miller claimed it.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
WILLIAMS'S Defence. I did not know she had it.
M'KENZIE - GUILTY. Aged 23.
WILLIAMS - GUILTY. Aged 30.
"A statute having passed subsequent to the commission of this offence, allowing the benefit of Clergy in these cases, provided the value of property stolen be under 15 l. The Learned Common Sergeant reserved this case for the consideration of the Twelve Judges to determine whether the prisoners are entitled to the benefit of the same."
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.

1172. WILLIAM MAY was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of July, one quart pot, value 18 d., the goods of John Abbey.
THOMAS DALE. I am pot-boy to the Black Cap, public house, Cambden-town. I followed the prisoner, and said I was informed he had some of my master's pots; he said he had not, and begged I would let him go. He gave me one of Mr. Clark's pots, and I took him to Mr. Clark. The officer found a quart pot of Mr. Abbey's on him.
JOHN DAVIS. I took him in charge, and pulled two pots out of his bosom.
JOHN ABBEY. I keep the Melville, public-house, in Greek-street - the pot is mine.

Confined Nine Months, and Publicly Whipped.

1173. PETER WELCH was indicted for stealing, on the 3d of August, one shirt, value 7 s.; two handkerchiefs, value 1 s., and one apron, value 1 s., the goods of Joseph Northern.
JOSEPH NORTHERN. I keep the Coach and Horses, at Knightsbridge . About eleven o'clock the prisoner came in and said he was a tailor, and asked if I could get him work. I found them at the tailor's, under the shop-board.

1174. JOHN SULLIVAN was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of July, one handkerchief, value 1 s., the goods of John Simpson, from his person.
JOHN SIMPSON. I am a jeweller, and live in Middle-row, Holborn. On the 4th of July I was in Covent Garden; a man touched me on the shoulder, and asked if I had lost my handkerchief; I felt and missed it. The prisoner was about one hundred yards off with a companion - we secured him.
WILLIAM COLTON. I am a broker, and live at Battle-bridge. On the day of the Westminster meeting I was at Covent Garden, and saw the prosecutor standing by a man who was selling a composition, and saw the prisoner with two more try several gentlemens' pockets. I saw him try Simpson's pocket, then go away and bring two more; then saw him take the handkerchief from the prosecutor's pocket. I asked him if he had lost anything - he said he had. When they saw me touch the prosecutor, two went one way, and the other another. I seized the prisoner, but did not find the handkerchief.
WILLIAM LEE. I am a Bow-street patrol. They were given into my charge - the one was discharged.
Prisoner's Defence. My mother left me in the market; the gentleman went by and took me.
GUILTY. Aged 11.
Transported for Seven Years. - Penitentiary .
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18200918-276]
1175. WILLIAM WELLS was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of July, 18 lbs. of lead, value 3 s., the goods of Thomas Wade, and fixed to a building of his.
CHARLES SPRAGG. I am agent to Mr. Thomas Wade, who has premises in Three-Fox-court, St. Sepulchre's - he does not live there. On the 10th of July the officer fetched me there; I found part of the lead stripped from the gutter of the roof; it appeared fresh cut; about 3 cwt. was stolen; they produced 18 lbs., which fitted the cart shed.
DANIEL ELLINGHAM. I am a patrol of St. Sepulchre's. On the 8th of July, about half-past three o'clock in the morning, I apprehended the prisoner in Three-Fox-court, in a ruinous place by the cart-shed - he had a trowel in his hand, and was spreading some dirt. I asked what he was doing, he said nothing, but moved off. I immediately kicked against the dirt, and found the lead secreted under it. I followed and overtook him in Long-lane; he begged to be let go, and said he did it through distress. I found a knife on him, which had marks of lead on it. I fitted it to the roof.
GUILTY. Aged 34.
Transported for Seven Years.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.

1180. DANIEL MACK was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of September, one watch, value 3 l.; one seal, value 6 d.; and one chain, value 1 d., the goods of John Mills, from his person.
JOHN MILLS. I live in Cornwell-street, St. George's in the East. On the 16th of August, between five and six o'clock in the evening, I was in Cornwell-street, returning from a walk, somebody came against me, snatched my watch from my fob, and went off with it. It was produced in three minutes.
TIMOTHY CALLAMAN. I live in Cow-lane, Shadwell. I was coming out of the Middlesex Society-school, and heard Mills calling Stop thief! my school-fellows and I followed the prisoner who was near Mr. Mills. I saw him throw the watch into a gutter, I picked it up and gave it to Mills.
WILLIAM HENRY HUNT. I am a constable. I secured the prisoner. Callaman said he was the man who threw the watch away.
(Property produced, and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 19.

[trialAccount t18200918-282]
1181. CHARLES CALLAGHAN was indicted for stealling, on the 4th of September, one watch, value 2 l.; one seal, value 5 s.; one chain, value 5 s., and one key, value 1 s., the goods of Robert Newby, from his person.
ROBERT NEWBY. I am a sawyer, and live in Richmond-place, Old-street. On the 4th of September, between four and five o'clock in the afternoon I was in Ball-yard, Golden-lane. I went there to call on a mate named Evans, who owed me some money; while I was disputing with him, he knocked me down. I was picked up by several people who stood round, and as they took me up, they took my watch from my fob. A little boy came up and said,
"You have lost your watch; I saw Jack Harris take it." He ran away. I have not recovered it.
THOMAS HILL. I keep a chandler's-shop in Little Arthur-street. I saw a crowd in Ball-yard as I stood at my shop, the prisoner and Harris were there, they ran out of the crowd together, and came up towards me. I knew them both perfectly well. In five or ten minutes, I heard the prosecutor had lost his watch. As they came towards me, opposite the Crown and Sceptre door, I saw Harris give the prisoner something which appeared to be a watch. They were about ten yards from me. I informed the constable.
JOSEPH PRINCE. I am an officer. I heard of the robbery the same day, and Hill gave me the account he has now. Tweedy and I went to the prisoner's lodgings, he was denied to us, we went up stairs, and found him concealed under the bed clothes, with all his clothes but his coat on, it was in the afternoon. I told him what Hill said, he said he knew nothing of it, he did not take it.
JOHN TWEEDY. I saw the prisoner and apprehended him. I knew him and Harris before, I have seen them in company.
RICHARD STEVENS. I am a shoemaker, and live in Ball-yard. I saw Harris very busy in picking the prosecutor up, and saw him draw the watch from his pocket, he would the chain round his fingers, and ran off. My lad ran after him. I have not seen him since, and did not see the prisoner.
Prisoner's Defence. I was not under the clothes.
GUILTY. Aged. 19.
Transported for Life.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18200918-283]
1182. JOSEPH BARNETT, HENRY BADMAN, and EDWARD WILLIAMS, were indicted for stealing, on the 11th of September, one snuff-box, value 20 s., the goods of William Smith, from his person.
WILLIAM SMITH. I keep the Stone Kitchen, public-house in the Tower. On the 11th of September, between four and five o'clock, I was on the Tower-wharf, there was a rowing-match, which brought a crowd round, and my snuffbox was stolen.
WALTER STONE. I am a constable of Tower Ward. I was on the wharf between four and five o'clock in the afternoon, and observed Barnet and Williams particularly active in looking at different peoples' pockets. Badman was with him, but I did not observe him do any thing. Just as the boats started there was a sudden rush. I observed four or five bad characters, and told a person to tell me if the prisoners left the crowd. In a few minutes I was informed they had left. I came up with them on the bridge which leads to the lawn. Barnett was nearest to me, I took him, and Lewis took the other two, who were further off. We took them to the Guard-house. Just as we entered I heard something drop, at that time Barnett and I were behind, the others had entered the guard-house. I found the snuff-box laying between Barnett and me, he said
"You know I did not drop it." I took him to the guard-house. Nobody was in custody but the prisoners.
JOHN LEWIS. I saw Badman and Williams on the wharf for a considerable time. I afterwards had information, and saw the prisoners together. I took Badman and Williams to the guard-house. I was before Stone, and was searching them at the time the snuff-box fell. Stone had not entered with Barnett.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
BARNETT'S Defence. The other prisoners were just before me.
BARNETT - GUILTY. Aged 18.
WILLIAMS - GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Life.
BADMAN - NOT GUILTY.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.

1186. MARY HOLLES was indicted for stealing, on the 2d of September, one stove-grate, value 8 s.; one lock, value 2 s.; four brass-handles, value 1 s., and three keys, value 1 s., the goods of Colin Forbes.
SECOND COUNT, stating them to be the goods of Ann Forbes.
THIRD COUNT, stating them to be the goods of Thomas Leman,
ANN FORBES. I live in Devonshire-street, Globe-fields. I do not know whether my husband is living, I have not seen him for fourteen years. I have a house No. 11, Dog-row, Bethnal-green. On the 31st of August, the prisoner came in company with another man to look at it; he agreed to take it, and if I would wait two hours they would bring me 5 l. as a deposit. This property was all fixed, except the three keys. They did not return - I went next, day all was right then. On Saturday morning, in consequence of information, I went to the house, and missed all the fixtures and three keys. Thomas Leman was my trustee for the house.
ELIZA VICKERS. I am servant to Mrs. Forbes. I shewed the prisoner the house, the things were all safe then and on the next day. On Saturday I missed the keys and fixtures.
WILLIAM WIGGETT. I am a sail-maker, and live in Dog-row. I was going up Dog-row, between five and six o'clock in the morning, and saw a quantity of people round the door of Forbes's house. I stopped, saw the door open, and in a few minutes the prisoner came out; I caught her in my arms, and held her until the officer came - she struggled to get away. I went into the house, and found the stove unfixed. Twenty-five shillings and four screws were found upon her, but no keys.
CHARLES BOLTON. I live in Dog-row. I was called up, and took her into custody; I found nobody else there, although the door was shut by a person inside when she came out. I found the stove in the passage, and four screws upon her, three of which fitted a door which had a lock taken off.
Prisoner's Defence. A person left a direction with my sister, for me to go to the house to work. A man brought me in, and told me to clean a room out; as there was no pail there, I went to fetch mine, and was stopped at the door.
GUILTY. Aged 32.
Transported for Seven Years.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18200918-288]
1187. ELIZA JOHNSTONE was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of August, two shoes, value 3 s., the goods of William Hayhow.
WILLIAM HAYHOW. I am a shoemaker, and live in New-road, St. George's in the East. On the 25th of August, Hart brought in some odd shoes to sell; I identified two of them to be mine. I have been repeatedly robbed. In consequence of what she said the prisoner was apprehended. She was a stranger.
WILLIAM SUMMERS. I am a constable. I was sent for, and Hart took me to Martha-street - I found the prisoner there. Hart said she sold her the shoes; the prisoner said a man of her acquaintance borrowed fourpence on them.I took her in charge. The prosecutor produced the fellows.
RACHAEL HART. I am a clothes-woman, and live in Petticoat-lane. I bought these shoes of the prisoner for 1 s. the same day. She said she lent a man a trifle of money on them. I asked for the fellows, she said he made them as patterns.

1188. HENRY GREEN, DAVID PLUMMER, and JOSEPH WATTS were indicted for stealing, on the 31st of July, six blankets, value 15 s., and four curtains, value 10 s., the goods of William Perkins; 100 lbs. of lead, and one copper, value 25 s., the goods of William Crowther, and fixed to his dwelling-house.
WILLIAM CROWTHER. The house this property was stolen from is mine. I live in Charles-street; Middlesex Hospital, next door to it. On Sunday afternoon, the 30th of July, about four o'clock, I observed the lead which covered the back-yard and had been fixed to it, was cut away, and put in the hall of the house; the copper was also unfixed, and put there. The person who inhabited the house had taken the key away. I found some blankets and curtains in the copper; the copper was tied in a blanket. About seven o'clock next morning, I found the prisoners in custody.
WILLIAM PERKINS. The blankets and curtains in the house are mine. I had lent them to the last tenant.
WILLIAM HOUSE. I am servant to Mr. Crowther. On Sunday evening he sent me and some more men to watch who would come; we waited in the opposite house. At three o'clock the officers left us, we remained, and at half-past five o'clock three men entered the house. We immediately went to the door, and found Plummer and Watts in the passage removing the copper; Green, who was the third, ran into the parlour and hid behind the door, I secured him; the others were secured. The lead had been cut up, and the mortar of the copper appeared fresh.
JOHN HUNTER. I am a coach-painter. I was upon the watch, and found the prisoners in the house. I secured Watts in the passage; he begged me not to ill-use him.
JAMES LYON. I secured Plummer in the passage.
WILLIAM HELY. I am servant to Mr. Crowther. I saw Green enter the house with a knot under his arm; the other prisoners let him in, they were inside. I informed the witnesses, they went in and secured them. They gave no account how they came there.
ROBERT CONNELLY. I am a labourer. I saw Green come out of the house with two more, on Sunday morning, at half-past seven o'clock, the day before the robbery.
JOHN JONES. The prisoners were brought to the watch-house with this property. I found a knife upon Green, and a crow-bar in the copper, which exactly fitted the door where it was forced.
GREEN'S Defence. I met Plummer in Oxford-street. A gentleman sent us to the house to fetch the goods. He went to get some beer, and we were secured in the house.
PLUMMER'S Defence. We met the gentleman, and he sent us to carry the load.
HENRY GREEN - GUILTY. Aged 22.
DAVID PLUMMER - GUILTY. Aged 30.
JOSEPH WATTS - GUILTY. Aged 32.
Transported for Seven Years.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.

[trialAccount t18200918-297]
1196. JOHN WILSON was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of August, from the person of John Dean, one pocketbook, value 1 s., and one 5 l. Bank note, his property.
JOHN DEAN. I live in Swallow-street. On the 30th of August, about five o'clock in the afternoon, I was in St. James's square when the Clerkenwell address was being presented to the Queen. The Queen's carriage came up; there was a very heavy pressure from the crowd; two females were before me, who cried for assistance; while my hands were up endeavouring to protect them, I felt something at my pocket, put my hands down and missed my pocket-book, which was safe a moment before; the prisoner was close to me; I instantly seized him, and said,
"You scoundrel, you have taken my pocket-book!" two men immediately seized him and asked me to let him go, but I held him. I found they were officers and I gave him up to them; one of them immediately pulled my pocket-book from his breeches.
JOHN FURZEMAN. I am an officer. I was in the square with Wales, seized the prisoner, and took the pocket-book from his breeches,
(Property produced and sworn to.)
JOHN WALES. I saw the pocket-book taken from his breeches.
Prisoner's Defence. I saw two men scrambling for it, and picked it up.
GUILTY. Aged 28.
Transported for Life.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18200918-298]
1197. WILLIAM WILLIAMS and ANN WILLIAMS were indicted for that they, on the 21st of July, in a public street and highway, in and upon Mary Busby, wilfully, maliciously, and feloniously did make an assault, with intent to spoil, burn, and deface her garments; and did wilfully, maliciously, and feloniously spoil, burn, and deface one shawl, value 10 s.; one gown, value 10 s.; one petticoat, value 3 s., and one pair of stockings, value 1 s., her property, and part of her garments on her person in wear, to her great damage.
MARY BUSBY. I live in Herod's-place, Well-street, Well-close-square. On the 21st of July, about half-past eleven o'clock at night, I was coming from a dancing-house next door to Mrs. Williams, in Dock-street; I had lived with her about four months before; we had quarelled before this happened. As I came out I saw her at her door, but did not see her son; she moved her hands towards me; I did not see anything; nobody but her was near me. I immediately felt a great pain at my ancle like burning; I went home took my stocking off, and found three or four holes burnt in it; my shawl was all over with the same sort of stuff, and had holes in it; my gown was the same, and my petticoat. In the morning I went to Lambeth-street and took out a warrant against her; the other prisoner came to me and said he would serve me ten times worse, and said he was behind her at the time.
FRANCIS FREEMAN. I am an officer of Lambeth-street. The prosecutrix came to me for a warrant and gave me her clothes, which I produce; the stockings appeared to be burnt with vitriol; all her clothes were burnt. I apprehended Mrs. Williams - she declared her innocence.
ANN WILLIAMS 'S Defence. I was washing very hard all that day, and never saw her. All this is done out of spite; she was a common girl.
SARAH SEABROOK. I live in Dock-street. Busby complained to me of being hurt, and shewed me her clothes; her ancle was burnt just like a pea; there was a great hole all down her petticoat.
WILLIAM WILLIAMS - NOT GUILTY.
ANN WILLIAMS - GUILTY. Aged 44.
Confined Six Months.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.

1202. WILLIAM DODD was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of August, 30 lbs. of lead, value 5 s., the goods of John Emmett.
JOHN EMMETT. I am a carpenter, and live at Pentonville.I lost the lend from a building, No. 11, Cloudsley-terrace, Islington.
WILLIAM EMMETT. I am the prosecutor's cousin. On Friday morning, the 5th of August, about six o'clock, I saw the lead safe, it laid in the house; when I came from dinner I saw the prisoner and another man walking about thirty yards from the house. I suspected the prisoner, as he appeared bulky. I followed them across the field and they dropped the lead.
WILLIAM BROWN. I am a plumber. I followed the men, and saw the prisoner throw down two pieces of lead and the other man one. I stopped the prisoner.
STEPHEN STANTON. I am a labourer. I saw the prisoner on the premises.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I heard the alarm, followed the cry, and was stopped.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Confined Six Months and Publicly Whipped.

1209. ANDREW MANN, WILLIAM LEMON, and PETER CAREY were indicted for stealing, on the 25th of August, 300 lbs. of lead, value 26 s., the goods of John Hinton Tritton, and fixed to his dwelling-house.
SECOND COUNT, stating it to be the goods of Samuel Baxter, and fixed to a building of his.
WILLIAM ATKINSON. I am a carpenter. I had the care of Mr. John Hinton Tritton 's house, No. 46, Mortimer-street, Marylebone. On the 24th of August, at seven o'clock in the evening, I saw the lead safe. About seven o'clock next morning I found it had been stripped off three dormers and part of the ridge. About three hundred weight had been stolen. Carey worked on the next premises shortly before. Mr. Tritton lived in the house at the time.
JOHN COOPER. I am a watchman of St. Marylebone. On the 25th of August I was calling one o'clock, andheard a rattle spring - somebody said some men were on the roof of No. 46, stealing lead. I ran down to No. 49, the people opened the door; I went upon the roof, and found Mann there. He said two men had been taking lead, but it was not him, for he came there to sleep.
Cross-examined by MR. HONE. Q. No. 47 is a ruinous house - A. The house next door to the prosecutor's is coming down - the door of it was shut.
THOMAS BOX. I am a watchman. I heard an alarm that thieves were at No. 46; I called two watchmen, and placed them at the back of the premises; the servants said some men were on the roof. I went into No. 45, and found Lemon on his hands and knees behind the door - he said he came there for lead to support his family. I gave him to a watchman while I searched the house, and found Carey in the chimney. I found the lead laying in the same room - it was fresh cut, and some torn off.
SAMUEL HILL. I am a constable. The prisoners were brought to the watch-house. The lead weighed 312 lbs. I compared it with the building, and it fitted exactly.
THOMAS READ. I am servant to Mr. Tritton. I went on the roof, and missed the lead. It appeared just taken. I found Mann and Cary in the next house.
LEMON'S Defence. I went there for shelter.
CAREY'S Defence. It rained, and I went for shelter.
MANN'S Defence. Being in debt I slept in old houses, and went to this house.
MANN - GUILTY. Aged 24.
LEMON - GUILTY. Aged 27.
CAREY - GUILTY. Aged 30.
Transported for Seven Years.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18200918-311]
1210. JOHN HALSEY and WILLIAM BERRY were indicted for stealing, on the 11th of July, one gown, value 7 s.; two bed-gowns, value 3 s.; one frock, value 3 s.; four pinafores, value 3 s.; five handkerchiefs, value 2 s., and one pair of stockings, value 1 s., the goods of Gerrard Rawes.
MARTHA RAWES. I am the wife of Gerrard Rawes, and live in Winchester-street, Pentonville. On the 11th of July, about three o'clock in the afternoon, I missed the things from my front parlour, they were in a bundle. I saw them safe at nine o'clock; there was a fore-court to the house, and I believe the window was open. I found them at Hatton-garden.
GEORGE TAYLOR. I am a constable. On the 11th of July, about eleven o'clock in the morning, I was by Pentonville-chapel, about three hundred yards from Mr. Rawes's, and saw the prisoners together. Berry had a basket with willow-shavings at the top. and Halsey had a bundle of wood in his hand. They passed me, and went down Southampton-street. As they passed me Halsey put the wood in his hat. I got Colton to assist me, we found them at the back of White Conduit-house together. Halsey then had a bundle under his arm, and Berry had the basket. Halsey stopped while Berry went to several gentlemen's houses. We searched them, and found a latchkey on Berry, and a bridle on Halsey. He said he picked it up in the field. The basket contained nothing but shavings in it, when I first saw him the basket was bulky, but when I apprehended him there were only a few shavings. Halsey told the Magistrate he was to have 1 s. to carry the basket to Old-street.
WILLIAM COLTON. I live at Battle-bridge. Taylor fetched me, we found the prisoners near White Conduit-fields. Halsey had the bundle under his arm, Berry was carrying the basket. He said the things were his own.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
HALSEY'S Defence. I saw a man with large whiskers, he wished me to carry the bundle to Old-street.
BERRY'S Defence. I was selling willow shavings.
HALSEY - GUILTY. Aged 20.
BERRY - GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Seven Years.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
