[trialAccount t18200918-313]
1212. THOMAS DORSET and GEORGE BERRY BRYAN, were indicted for stealing, on the 17th of September, one scarf, value 2 s., and one shawl, value 2 s., the goods of Thomas William Dobby.
THOMAS WILLIAM DOBBY. I am a cutler, and live on Great Saffron-hill. I was riding down Ball's Pond-road in a chaise-cart, with my wife and child, the shawl and scarf hung on the tail of the cart. Ward called out that they were stolen. I turned round, and saw Dorset about twenty yards off attempting to conceal them under his coat. He saw me, and let them fall. Bryan was with him. I picked it up, and they ran across the fields.
MARY WARD. I live in Lower Queen-street, Islington. I was sitting down in Ball's Pond-road, and saw Bryan give Dorset a parcel, they were just at the prosecutor's cart. I saw them come from the cart-tail, and saw the fringe of the shawl at the time he gave it to the other prisoner. Dorset endeavoured to hide it. I ran and told Mr. Dobby. Dorset then dropped it, and both ran across the fields. I saw them in custody next day; I am sure they are the persons.
THOMAS KINSEY. I live in Ward's-place, Islington.I saw Bryan tread on the wheel, take the things out of the cart, and give them to Dorset; they ran away, and I saw Dorset drop it; they ran across the fields. Mr. Dobby pursued them, but they got away. I saw them next day at the office, and am sure they are the persons.
JOHN UPTON. I am an an officer. I took Dorset the same night at Islington, about nine o'clock, and took Bryan next day.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
BRYON - GUILTY. Aged 15.
Transported for Seven Years.
DORSET - GUILTY. Aged 16.
Confined Three Months, and Whipped.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.

1213. ELIZA COLLETT was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of June, one watch, value 5 l.; one seal, value 14 s., and one key, value 7 s., the goods of John Dobson, from his person.
JOHN DOBSON. I live in St. George's-market. On the 10th of June, about eleven o'clock at night, I met the prisoner in Stratford-place, Oxford-street. I went to a house in George-street, St. Giles's. About six o'clock in the morning I missed her and my watch. I found the door fastened outside, and could not get out.
ANN COX. I live in George-street. I let the prisoner and the prosecutor in, he was sober. The prisoner rang the bell, and went to get something to drink. She said she had left her bonnet and stays behind her. I did not see her again till she was apprehended.
ALEXANDER FRASER. I am a watchman. On the 27th of August, Dobson pointed the prisoner out to me. I secured her, she said she never saw him.
Prisoner's Defence. I saw a woman looking over the partition of the room, but whether she got over and took it I do not know.
ANN COX re-examined. The partition goes up almost to the cieling. Nobody can get over.
GUILTY. Aged 36.
Confined One Year.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18200918-315]
1214. JOHN BRENT was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of September, one watch, value 20 s., the goods of Samuel Duckman, from his person.
SAMUEL DUCKMAN. I am a sailor. On the 17th of September, about half-past eight o'clock at night, I was in King David's-lane, going to a fire there; as I passed over the shafts of the engines, the prisoner and three others stood against the wall - they came behind, pushed me along, and as I turned round, whipped my watch from my fob. The prisoner was nearest to me - he got behind two others. I seized him, and the others ran off.
Cross-examined by MR. LAW. Q. You did not see him take it - A. No, but he was one of them, and he was the nearest to me; the others were not near enough to take it.
JOHN DUNCAN. I am a headborough. The prosecutor siezed the prisoner, and gave him to me; he said
"I have not got your watch, search me." I saw two others with him.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Transported for Seven Years. - Penitentiary .
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.

1216. THOMAS GILMAN and JOHN DAVIS were indicted for stealing, on the 2d of July, one watch, value 15 l.; one chain, value 2 l.; two seals, value 1 l., and two keys, value 7 s., the goods of James Gunn Harris, from his person.
JAMES GUNN HARRIS. I am a traveller, and live in Fenchurch street. On the 2d of July, about half-past twelve o'clock at night, I was in Whitecross-street. I heard a cry of murder! and found a great crowd collected. A number of people were ill-using the watchman. I knew him, and helped him to secure two people, and take them to the watch-house. I lost my watch in the crowd - I did not perceive it taken from me, it was safe when I entered the crowd - I did not miss it until the witness informed me of it. I am confident that Gilman hustled me twice in the mob. I found him at the watch-house on the Friday night following, and was certain of him the moment his hat was put on.
BENJAMIN WHOWALL. I work for Mr. Gade, a trunk-maker, in Coleman-street. I saw the prosecutor come into the crowd, and saw the two prisoners come up; I am certain of them, they were about six feet apart. Gilman hallooed out
"Tom" and pointed to Davis, who then nodded to him - Gilman pointed to the prosecutor's watch, his seals hung out. Two women were being taken to the watch-house, and as they were being led through the posts at the corner of Long's-buildings, Gilman said to Davis
"Come on!" Davis followed him; they went up the passage, and got close to Harris; a third person was then with them, Davis was going on the right side, Gilman pushed him, and said
"Go along." He then went to the left of Harris, Gilman was close behind, he pushed Harris behind, and the others pushed him on each side. Gilman put out his hand and took out the watch. He said
"Come on!" All three pushed out of the crowd, and went away. I followed them, as I wished to see where they went to. Davis asked Gilman to go down Chequer-alley, he objected, and they went first up Cowheel-alley. I looked at both full in the face; Gilman turned round, and said
"What are you nosing at." I went up the street, tucked my apron up, went to the watch-house, and told Harris. I described them to the watchman, and on Monday night pointed Davis out at the corner of Chequer-alley; the officer took him. On Wednesday I pointed Gilman out, and he was taken; he had a new hat on then, but when he committed the robbery he had a shabby one. When an old hat was put upon him, Harris swore to his being in the mob.
Prisoner GILMAN. Q. Why not take me at the time - A. I could not take three. I saw nobody to assist me.
JAMES HANLEY. I am a constable. I went with Harris and Whowall on Monday night. He pointed Davis out, and I took him.
JOSEPH PRINCE. I am an officer. On the 4th of July, Whowall pointed Gilman out. I took him; he made great resistance. I was obliged to handcuff him.
JOHN TWEEDY. I and Prince apprehended Gilman.
Davis's Defence. I am innocent.
THOMAS GILMAN - GUILTY. Aged 19.
JOHN DAVIS - GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Life.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18200918-318]
1218. JOHN JULIER was indicted for stealing, on the 3d of July, one watch, value 10 l.; two seals, value 4 l.; two keys, value 1 s.; one ring, value 3 s., and one piece of ribbon, value 1 d., the goods of Nicholas Dechemant, from his person.
NICHOLAS DECHEMANT. I live in Frith-street, Soho. On the 3d of July I was in a pastrycook's-shop in Oxford-street. The City procession was returning from presenting an address to the Queen. I remained in the shop near the door; some people came up, pushed me, and snatched my watch. I have not recovered it.
WILLIAM MILES. I am a carpenter. I stopped by the pastrycook's window as the procession passed. The prisoner was running with four or five behind him. He stopped directly he came to the prosecutor, looked round, and they all stopped in the centre of the door, made a rush, and I saw the prisoner snatch the prosecutor's watch. I am certain of his person. I told the prosecutor of it. Jones and I ran, caught the prisoner in a public-house in Oxford-street, and gave him in charge.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Who do you work for - A. Mr. Ward, of Pimlico. I am going to the East Indies. I live at Peckham. I lost sight of him, but I am certain he is the man.
Prisoner's Defence. He said at the office that he never lost sight of me. He said he would transport me to get his expences.
GUILTY. Aged 25.
Transported for Life.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18200918-319]
1219. MARY WEST was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of August, one necklace, value 5 s., the goods of Philip Hubie, from the person of Mary Hubie .
FRANCES HUBIE. I am the wife of Philip Hubie. I sent Clark out with my daughter, Mary; she had a necklace on, but returned without one. I live in Brick-lane.
ELLEN CLARK. I took the child out between three and four o'clock; the prisoner and another girl came behind us in Brick-lane, and then came before us; I am sure of her person; the prisoner then leant over my shoulder; I saw her hand go away from the child's neck, and missed the necklace; I charged her with stealing it; the other girl ran away; a gentleman seized the prisoner.
HENRY EDLIN. I am a butcher. I heard the girl scream out that the prisoner had took the necklace; I saw the prisoner give something to the other girl, who ran away immediately. I secured West and gave her in charge.
Prisoner's Defence. I was on the other side of the way.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Confined Six Months.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.

1220. MARIA HARVEY was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of July, one purse, value 1 d. and 13 s. in monies numbered, the property of Peter Croiley, from his person.
PETER CROILEY. I am a book-keeper, and live in Duke-street, Portman-square. On the 29th of July, about eleven o'clock at night, I was met in Oxford-street by a party of Irishmen, and was knocked down; my pockets were rifled of a silk handkerchief and other trifles; I received some severe blows; on recovering myself I found the prisoner by my side, and accompanied her to a house in Orchard-street, up three pair of stairs; I was in the same room with her and another woman; I went there for no improper purpose. I began to suspect all was not right, and pulled my purse out; the prisoner immediately snatched it from me, the candles were put out, and I was told to get down stairs or they would break my neck; I got down with some difficulty, and immediately pointed the house out to the watchman; we went in and I pointed the prisoner out; she was secured, and the purse found in the house, in the room I was robbed in; I described it before I saw it.
THOMAS ALLSOP. I was constable of the night. I was at the corner of Orchard-street; the prosecutor fetched me; I got two watchmen and found the prisoner and two other women in the three pair room; he pointed the prisoner out, and gave this account. I found the purse under the bed between the sacking and mattress, empty; he described it before he saw it.
(Purse produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I never saw him.
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Transported for Life.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18200918-321]
1221. JOHN FITZGERALD was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of August, one handkerchief, value 4 s., the goods of a certain man unknown, from his person.
CHARLES READ. I am a constable. On the 5th of August, about half-past eleven o'clock at night, as the people came out of the English Opera-house, I saw the prisoner, in company with another, try several gentlemen's pockets; they followed two gentlemen; I saw the prisoner take the handkerchief from one of their pockets, and put his hand behind to give it to the other; I seized him and took it from his hand; a scuffle ensued but I kept him; the gentleman came to the watch-house, but I do not know where he is, or his name, or where he lives; he is an officer, and was going to France.
THOMAS HUNT. I was with Read, and saw him take the handkerchief from the prisoner's hand; the gentleman said his name was Prince or Price, I do not know which; he gave no Christian name.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Life.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
[trialAccount t18200918-322]
1122. MARGARET WALL was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of August, one watch, value 3 l., and one handkerchief, value 1 s., the goods of Joseph Drury, from his person.
JOSEPH DRURY. I am a copper-plate printer, and live in Whalebone-court, Coleman-street. On the 10th of August,about ten o'clock at night, I met the prisoner, she asked me to go home with her, I said I had no money. She said she could pawn my watch in the morning. I went, she put out the light, took my watch from under my head, and went away. A man and woman came up, and said if I did not leave the house he would give me in charge. I went out, saw her running, and told the watchman, who took her. I was sober.
RICHARD PLUNKET. The prisoner was brought to the watch-house. I found nothing on her. I think the prosecutor was intoxicated, he had been brought to the watch-house two nights before drunk, and charged me with robbing him of a 1 l. note.
NOT GUILTY.
Third Middlesex Jury, before J. Vaillant, Esq.
[trialAccount t18200918-323]
1123. JOHN DOUGLAS was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of July, two cushions, value 10 s., the goods of Robert Silk.
LAWRENCE BARRY. I am servant to Mr. Robert Silk, a coachmaker in Long-Acre. I saw the prisoner leave the shop with three cushions under his arm. I followed and secured him with them. He said his master sent him for them.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 53.
Confined Six Months.
Third Middlesex Jury, before J. Vaillant, Esq.
[trialAccount t18200918-324]
1124. EDWARD WELCH was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of September, one rug, value 5 s., the goods of Thomas Davis.
THOMAS DAVIS. I am pass-master for the County of Middlesex, and live in Vine-street, Liquorpond-street. In the morning, as the waggon was loading to go to Bristol, my man insisted on searching the prisoner's bag, we were taking him to Bristol. The rug was found in it.
GEORGE BROWN. I suspected the paupers. I missed a rug, and found it in the prisoner's bag.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. A countryman told me to take it.
GUILTY. Aged 39.
Transported for Seven Years.
Third Middlesex Jury, before J. Vaillant, Esq.
[trialAccount t18200918-325]
1225. WILLIAM SPENCER was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of August, one axe, value 6 s., the goods of James Payne.
JAMES PAYNE. I am a blacksmith, and live at Wilsden. On the 14th of August I lost two axes from my cupboard. The prisoner worked for me as a carpenter at the time. On the 17th of September I found one of them in Atkins's possession.
Cross-examined by MR. LAW. Q. Can you swear to the axe - A. Yes; I know it by a flaw in the make, and I am certain of it.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
JOSEPH ATKINS. I am a labourer. I bought the axe of the prisoner. He said his brother sent it him out of the country, but it was too heavy for him. I gave him 3 s. for it.
Cross-examined. Q. How far is the public-house from the prosecutor's - A. About one hundred yards.
Prisoner's Defence. I found it wrapped up in a hay-band, about one hundred and fifty yards from the prosecutor's.
GUILTY. Aged 35.
Fined One Shilling, and Discharged.
Third Middlesex Jury, before J. Vaillant, Esq.
